   Fix a promised near pair \((p,q)\), and put \(\sigma_0=\sigma(q)\).  Since
   \(\alpha_t(q,\omega)\) is supported on \(O(q,\omega)\), every terminal with
   positive responsibility satisfies \(B_t\subseteq B(q,\omega)\).  Thus
   \(b_p(B(q,\omega))\ge b_p(B_t)\), and by \((OD1)\),
   \[
      e^{-b_p(B(q,\omega))}
      \le
      \sum_{t\in T_{\sigma_0}}
      \alpha_t(q,\omega)e^{-b_p(B_t)} .
   \]
   Therefore
   \[
   \begin{aligned}
      \E_\omega\!\left[
         R_{p,q}(\omega)e^{-b_p(B(q,\omega))}
      \right]
      &\le
      \sum_{t\in T_{\sigma_0}}
      e^{-b_p(B_t)}
      \E_\omega\!\left[
         R_{p,q}(\omega)\alpha_t(q,\omega)
      \right]  \\
      &\le
      \sum_{t\in T_{\sigma_0}}
      U_{p,\sigma_0,t}e^{-b_p(B_t)}
      \le\tau .
   \end{aligned}
   \]

   For the work bound, nonnegativity of the point-label intensities gives
   \[
      b_x(B(q,\omega))
      =
      b_x\!\left(\bigcup_{t\in O(q,\omega)}B_t\right)
      \le
      \sum_{t\in O(q,\omega)}b_x(B_t).
   \]
   Hence
   \[
   \begin{aligned}
      &\E_\omega\!\left[
         \sum_{v\in W(q,\omega)}d_v
         +\sum_xb_x(B(q,\omega))
      \right]  \\
      &\qquad\le
      \sum_{v\in V_{\sigma_0}}
      V^{\rm state}_{\sigma_0,v}d_v
      +
      \sum_{t\in T_{\sigma_0}}
      V^{\rm out}_{\sigma_0,t}\sum_xb_x(B_t)
      \le\ell .
   \end{aligned}
   \]
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Scalar redundancy water filling]
   \label{lem:scalar-redundancy-waterfill}
   Work in the Poisson multi-assignment model of
   \cref{lem:poisson-rate-coverage}, but fix the label reporter
   \(\mathcal A(q,Z)\) and the point assignment laws \(\mu_x\).  Let
   \(\mathcal N\) be a finite set of tilted near-pair constraints, and attach
   nonnegative prices \(\lambda_{p,q}\) to them.  For \(s\ge0\), define the
   scalar residual loss and marginal coverage of point \(p\) by
   \[
      F_p(s)
      =
      \sum_{q:\,(p,q)\in\mathcal N}
      \lambda_{p,q}
      \E_Z\!\left[
         R_{p,q}(Z)
         \exp\!\left(-s\,\mu_p(\mathcal A(q,Z))\right)
      \right],
      \tag{RW1}
   \]
   \[
      D_p(s)
      =
      \sum_{q:\,(p,q)\in\mathcal N}
      \lambda_{p,q}
      \E_Z\!\left[
         R_{p,q}(Z)\,
         \mu_p(\mathcal A(q,Z))
         \exp\!\left(-s\,\mu_p(\mathcal A(q,Z))\right)
      \right].
      \tag{RW2}
   \]
   For a storage price \(\gamma>0\), the optimal replication budgets for the
   priced scalar problem
   \[
      \inf_{a_p\ge0}
      \left\{
      \sum_p F_p(a_p)+\gamma\sum_pa_p
      \right\}
      \tag{RW3}
   \]
   are independent over points and satisfy
   \[
      a_p=0\quad\text{if }D_p(0)\le\gamma,
      \tag{RW4}
   \]
   while every positive optimum satisfies the water-level equation
   \[
      D_p(a_p)=\gamma .
      \tag{RW5}
   \]
   If \(D_p\) has flat intervals, any point in the corresponding solution
   interval is optimal.

   Equivalently, for a hard total storage budget
   \[
      \sum_pa_p\le A,
      \tag{RW6}
   \]
   an optimum is obtained by choosing a water level \(\gamma\ge0\) so that the
   solutions of \((RW4)\)--\((RW5)\) have total budget \(A\), unless the
   unconstrained \(\gamma=0\) optimum already uses less.  Thus extra
   replication is assigned exactly to points whose current marginal tilted
   coverage \(D_p(a_p)\) is largest.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   Since \(0\le\mu_p(\mathcal A(q,Z))\le1\), differentiating under the
   expectation is justified by dominated convergence.  The function \(F_p\) is
   convex and nonincreasing, and
   \[
      F_p'(s)=-D_p(s).
   \]
   The function \(D_p\) is nonincreasing in \(s\).  The objective in \((RW3)\)
   separates over \(p\), so \(a_p\) minimizes \(F_p(s)+\gamma s\) on
   \([0,\infty)\).  The right derivative at \(0\) is \(\gamma-D_p(0)\).  If it
   is nonnegative, convexity makes \(s=0\) optimal.  Otherwise every positive
   minimizer must have zero subgradient, which is exactly \((RW5)\), with the
   usual interval convention when the convex function has a flat minimum.

   The constrained statement is the KKT form of the same convex program with a
   Lagrange multiplier \(\gamma\) for \((RW6)\).  If the storage constraint is
   active, complementary slackness chooses the water level for which
   \(\sum_pa_p=A\); if it is inactive, the multiplier is zero.  The marginal
   interpretation follows directly from \((RW2)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite rate-coverage feasibility is convex]
   \label{lem:finite-rate-coverage-convex}
   Fix a finite label universe \(\mathcal A\), a finite set of resident points
   \(S\), a finite promised query set \(\mathcal Q\), and a finite set of near
   witnesses \(\mathcal N\subseteq S\times\mathcal Q\).  For each
   \(q\in\mathcal Q\), let \(\nu_q\) be a probability law on an outcome space
   \(\Omega_q\), and let the retained reporter map each
   \(\omega\in\Omega_q\) to a label set \(A_q(\omega)\subseteq\mathcal A\).
   For each \((p,q)\in\mathcal N\), let
   \(r_{p,q}(\omega)\ge0\) be an integrable tilted witness weight.  Given
   targets \(\tau_{p,q}>0\), \(\ell_q>0\), and \(S_0>0\), consider the intensity
   variables \(b_{x,a}\ge0\) and the constraints
   \[
      M_{p,q}(b)
      :=
      \int r_{p,q}(\omega)
      \exp\!\left(-\sum_{a\in A_q(\omega)}b_{p,a}\right)
      d\nu_q(\omega)
      \le \tau_{p,q},
      \tag{FC1}
   \]
   \[
      L_q(b)
      :=
      \int
      \sum_{x\in S}\sum_{a\in A_q(\omega)}b_{x,a}
      d\nu_q(\omega)
      \le \ell_q,
      \tag{FC2}
   \]
   and
   \[
      \sum_{x\in S}\sum_{a\in\mathcal A}b_{x,a}\le S_0 .
      \tag{FC3}
   \]
   The feasible set is convex.

   Moreover, infeasibility has the following dual certificate: there exist
   nonnegative multipliers \(\lambda_{p,q}\), \(\theta_q\), and \(\gamma\), not
   all zero, such that
   \[
   \begin{aligned}
      \inf_{b\ge0}\Bigg\{
      &\sum_{(p,q)\in\mathcal N}\lambda_{p,q}M_{p,q}(b)
       +\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\theta_qL_q(b)
       +\gamma\sum_{x,a}b_{x,a}
      \Bigg\} \\
      &>
      \sum_{(p,q)\in\mathcal N}\lambda_{p,q}\tau_{p,q}
      +\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\theta_q\ell_q
      +\gamma S_0 .
   \end{aligned}
      \tag{FC4}
   \]
   Conversely, any multipliers satisfying \((FC4)\) prove infeasibility.
   The infimum in \((FC4)\) separates over data points.  If
   \[
      c_a=
      \gamma+
      \sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}\theta_q
      \nu_q\{\omega:\ a\in A_q(\omega)\},
      \tag{FC5}
   \]
   then the left side of \((FC4)\) equals
   \[
      \sum_{x\in S}
      \inf_{b_x\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{\mathcal A}}
      \left\{
      \sum_{q:\,(x,q)\in\mathcal N}
      \lambda_{x,q}
      \int r_{x,q}(\omega)
      e^{-\sum_{a\in A_q(\omega)}b_{x,a}}\,d\nu_q(\omega)
      +
      \sum_{a\in\mathcal A}c_ab_{x,a}
      \right\}.
      \tag{FC6}
   \]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   Each function \(M_{p,q}\) is convex because it is an integral of the
   exponential of an affine function of \(b\).  The load and storage
   constraints are linear.  Hence the feasible set is convex.

   If a feasible \(b\) exists, then for every nonnegative multiplier vector the
   weighted sum on the left of \((FC4)\), evaluated at that \(b\), is at most
   the weighted target on the right.  Therefore \((FC4)\) is impossible.

   Conversely, suppose the finite system is infeasible.  Let \(F(b)\) be the
   vector of violations
   \[
      \left(M(b)-\tau,\ L(b)-\ell,\ \sum_{x,a}b_{x,a}-S_0\right),
   \]
   and form the upper hull
   \[
      \mathcal C=\{F(b)+u:\ b\ge0,\ u\ge0\}.
   \]
   The system is feasible exactly when \(0\in\mathcal C\).  The set
   \(\mathcal C\) is convex: each miss coordinate is convex, the load and
   storage coordinates are linear, and adding the nonnegative orthant converts
   convex-coordinate interpolation into an upper hull.  It is also closed.  If
   \(F(b_n)+u_n\) converges, then the storage coordinate bounds
   \(\sum_{x,a}b_{n,x,a}\) from above, since \(u_n\ge0\); a subsequence of
   \(b_n\) therefore converges in the finite-dimensional nonnegative orthant,
   and dominated convergence for the miss integrals gives the limiting point of
   \(\mathcal C\).

   Since the system is infeasible, \(0\notin\mathcal C\).  Strict separation of
   the point \(0\) from the closed convex set \(\mathcal C\) gives a vector of
   multipliers whose inner product is positive on all of \(\mathcal C\).  The
   upper-hull property forces all multiplier coordinates to be nonnegative.
   Writing these coordinates as \(\lambda_{p,q}\), \(\theta_q\), and \(\gamma\)
   gives
   \[
      \inf_{b\ge0}
      \left(
      \sum_{(p,q)}\lambda_{p,q}(M_{p,q}(b)-\tau_{p,q})
      +\sum_q\theta_q(L_q(b)-\ell_q)
      +\gamma\left(\sum_{x,a}b_{x,a}-S_0\right)
      \right)>0,
   \]
   which is \((FC4)\).

   Finally, the load and storage terms contribute
   \[
      \sum_{x,a}
      \left(
         \gamma+
         \sum_q\theta_q\nu_q\{\omega:\ a\in A_q(\omega)\}
      \right)b_{x,a}.
   \]
   The miss terms for witness \((p,q)\) involve only the variables
   \(b_{p,a}\).  Therefore the Lagrangian infimum separates by point, giving
   \((FC6)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Reduced-cost rule for adding one rate label]
   \label{lem:rate-coverage-column-gain}
   Work in the setting of \cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-convex}.  Fix
   nonnegative dual multipliers \(\lambda,\theta,\gamma\), and fix current
   intensities \(b\) on a retained label universe \(\mathcal A\).  A proposed
   new label \(a_*\) is specified, for each query \(q\), by the event
   \[
      h_q(\omega)=\mathbf 1\{a_*\in A_q^+(\omega)\}\in\{0,1\},
   \]
   where \(A_q^+(\omega)\) is the reporter after adding \(a_*\).  Define its
   dual load-storage price
   \[
      c_*=
      \gamma+\sum_q\theta_q\int h_q(\omega)\,d\nu_q(\omega),
      \tag{CG0}
   \]
   and, for a point \(x\), its residual tilted witness value
   \[
      G_x(a_*)
      =
      \sum_{q:\,(x,q)\in\mathcal N}
      \lambda_{x,q}
      \int
      r_{x,q}(\omega)h_q(\omega)
      \exp\!\left(
         -\sum_{a\in A_q(\omega)}b_{x,a}
      \right)d\nu_q(\omega).
      \tag{CG1}
   \]
   If \(x\) is assigned intensity \(s\ge0\) to this new label, with all other
   intensities fixed, then the dual Lagrangian changes by
   \[
      \Delta_{x,a_*}(s)
      =
      G_x(a_*)\bigl(e^{-s}-1\bigr)+c_*s .
      \tag{CG2}
   \]
   Consequently the best one-column update has the closed form
   \[
      \inf_{s\ge0}\Delta_{x,a_*}(s)
      =
      \begin{cases}
      0, & G_x(a_*)\le c_*,\\[2mm]
      c_*-G_x(a_*)+c_*\log\!\bigl(G_x(a_*)/c_*\bigr),
         & G_x(a_*)>c_*>0,\\[2mm]
      -G_x(a_*), & c_*=0,
      \end{cases}
      \tag{CG3}
   \]
   where the middle case is achieved by
   \(s=\log(G_x(a_*)/c_*)\).  In particular, a label with
   \(G_x(a_*)>c_*\) is a rigorous improving column for the dual obstruction,
   while any obstruction that remains valid after adding a whole candidate label
   class must satisfy
   \[
      G_x(a)\le
      \gamma+\sum_q\theta_q\Pr_{\nu_q}[a\in A_q(\omega)]
      \tag{CG4}
   \]
   for every candidate label \(a\) and every data point \(x\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   Only witness constraints with first coordinate \(x\) depend on the new
   intensity \(s\).  On the event \(h_q(\omega)=1\), the miss exponential for
   witness \((x,q)\) is multiplied by \(e^{-s}\); on the complement it is
   unchanged.  Therefore the total change in the weighted miss terms is
   \[
      (e^{-s}-1)
      \sum_{q:\,(x,q)\in\mathcal N}
      \lambda_{x,q}
      \int r_{x,q}(\omega)h_q(\omega)
      e^{-\sum_{a\in A_q(\omega)}b_{x,a}}\,d\nu_q(\omega),
   \]
   which is the first term of \((CG2)\).  The load and storage terms add
   \(c_*s\) by \((CG0)\).

   The one-variable function
   \(G(e^{-s}-1)+cs\) is convex.  If \(G\le c\), its derivative at \(0\) is
   nonnegative, so \(s=0\) is optimal.  If \(G>c>0\), the derivative vanishes at
   \(s=\log(G/c)\), giving the middle line of \((CG3)\).  If \(c=0\), the
   infimum is obtained as \(s\to\infty\) and equals \(-G\).  Thus
   \(G_x(a_*)>c_*\) gives a strictly negative reduced-cost update.  Conversely,
   if a nonnegative dual vector is a valid obstruction after a candidate label
   has been added, no such single-coordinate update can reduce its Lagrangian,
   which gives \((CG4)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Whole-bucket reduced gain]
   \label{cor:whole-bucket-reduced-gain}
   In the setting of \cref{lem:rate-coverage-column-gain}, suppose a proposed
   label \(a_*\) may receive arbitrary nonnegative intensities
   \(s_x=b_{x,a_*}\) for all points \(x\in S\).  With \(c_*\) and \(G_x(a_*)\)
   as in \((CG0)\)--\((CG1)\), the best simultaneous update has value
   \[
      \inf_{s_x\ge0}
      \sum_{x\in S}
      \left[
         G_x(a_*)\bigl(e^{-s_x}-1\bigr)+c_*s_x
      \right]
      =
      -\sum_{x\in S}\Phi_{c_*}(G_x(a_*)),
      \tag{BG1}
   \]
   where the reduced-gain function is
   \[
      \Phi_c(G)=
      \begin{cases}
      0, & G\le c,\\[1mm]
      G-c-c\log(G/c), & G>c>0,\\[1mm]
      G, & c=0.
      \end{cases}
      \tag{BG2}
   \]
   The minimizing assignment is
   \[
      s_x=\log(G_x(a_*)/c_*)_+
      \tag{BG3}
   \]
   when \(c_*>0\), with the evident \(s_x\to\infty\) limiting convention on
   coordinates with \(c_*=0<G_x(a_*)\).  Thus the scalar
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}(a_*)=
      \sum_{x\in S}\Phi_{c_*}(G_x(a_*))
      \tag{BG4}
   \]
   is exactly the best dual-improvement supplied by the proposed bucket label.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
   After the reporter event of \(a_*\) is fixed, the Lagrangian change is the
   sum over \(x\) of the one-coordinate changes in \((CG2)\).  There is no
   coupling between the variables \(s_x\), because storage and reported load are
   charged per point-label incidence.  Applying \((CG3)\) independently to each
   point gives \((BG1)\)--\((BG3)\), and \((BG4)\) is just the negative of the
   minimized Lagrangian change.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Variational form of reduced-gain separation]
   \label{lem:reduced-gain-variational-separation}
   Work in the setting of \cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-convex}, with a
   current finite active set \(E\), nonnegative dual multipliers
   \((\lambda,\theta,\gamma)\), and current active intensities \(b^E\).  A
   proposed label \(a\) is represented by measurable reporter events
   \(h_{q,a}(\omega)=\mathbf 1\{a\in A_q^+(\omega)\}\).  Define its dual price
   \[
      c_a=\gamma+\sum_q\theta_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)\,d\nu_q(\omega).
   \]
   For each witness \((x,q)\), define the residual tilted density
   \[
      W^E_{x,q}(\omega)
      =
      \lambda_{x,q}r_{x,q}(\omega)
      \exp\!\left(
         -\sum_{a'\in A_q(\omega)}b^E_{x,a'}
      \right),
      \tag{VG0}
   \]
   with the convention that \(W^E_{x,q}=0\) when \((x,q)\notin\mathcal N\).
   Put
   \[
      G_x^E(a)=
      \sum_q\int W^E_{x,q}(\omega)h_{q,a}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega),
   \]
   and
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)=\sum_x\Phi_{c_a}(G_x^E(a)).
   \]
   For an intensity profile \(s=(s_x)_{x\in S}\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^S\), put
   \[
      \|s\|_1=\sum_xs_x
   \]
   and define the signed coverage surplus of a candidate label \(a\) by
   \[
   \begin{aligned}
      \Psi_E(a;s)
      &=
      \sum_q
      \int
      h_{q,a}(\omega)
      \left[
         \sum_x(1-e^{-s_x})W^E_{x,q}(\omega)
         -\theta_q\|s\|_1
      \right]d\nu_q(\omega)  \\
      &\qquad -\gamma\|s\|_1 ,
   \end{aligned}
      \tag{VG1}
   \]
   Then
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)
      =
      \sup_{s\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^S}\Psi_E(a;s).
      \tag{VG2}
   \]
   Consequently, \(a\) has positive reduced gain if and only if there is an
   intensity profile \(s\) for which the signed weighted coverage problem
   \[
      \Psi_E(a;s)>0
      \tag{VG3}
   \]
   succeeds.

   In particular, an implicit label family \(\mathcal U\) has no positive-gain
   label exactly when
   \[
      \sup_{a\in\mathcal U}\Psi_E(a;s)\le0
      \qquad\text{for every }s\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^S .
      \tag{VG4}
   \]
   Thus a separator for signed weighted coverage objectives of the form
   \((VG1)\), together with any exact outer maximization over \(s\), implements
   the reduced-gain separator of
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}.  Approximate separation is also
   sound in the one-sided sense: any returned label with verified
   \(\Psi_E(a;s)>0\) is an improving column, while a no-positive certificate
   must certify \((VG4)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   For \(c,G\ge0\), the reduced-gain function satisfies
   \[
      \Phi_c(G)=\sup_{s\ge0}\{G(1-e^{-s})-cs\}.
      \tag{VG5}
   \]
   Indeed, if \(G\le c\), the derivative \(Ge^{-s}-c\) is nonpositive at
   \(s=0\) and stays nonpositive, so the optimum is \(0\).  If \(G>c>0\), the
   optimum is at \(s=\log(G/c)\) and has value
   \(G-c-c\log(G/c)\).  If \(c=0\), the supremum is \(G\), approached as
   \(s\to\infty\).

   Applying \((VG5)\) independently for each point gives
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)
      =
      \sup_{s_x\ge0}
      \sum_x
      \left[
         G_x^E(a)(1-e^{-s_x})-c_a s_x
      \right].
      \tag{VG6}
   \]
   Substitute
   \[
      G_x^E(a)=
      \sum_q\int W^E_{x,q}(\omega)h_{q,a}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega)
   \]
   and
   \[
      c_a=\gamma+\sum_q\theta_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega).
   \]
   Rearranging the finite sums and integrals in \((VG6)\) gives exactly
   \((VG1)\), proving \((VG2)\).

   The positive-gain equivalence follows immediately from \((VG2)\).  Taking
   the supremum over \(a\in\mathcal U\) gives \((VG4)\).  Finally, when a
   candidate label and profile satisfy \((VG3)\), equation \((VG2)\) implies
   \(\operatorname{Gain}_E(a)>0\), so the verified label is an improving column
   by \cref{cor:whole-bucket-reduced-gain}.  Conversely, a no-positive answer
   is valid only if it proves the universal inequality \((VG4)\), which is
   precisely the no-gain hypothesis used by
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Layer-cake coverage separation]
   \label{lem:layer-cake-reduced-gain-separation}
   Work in the notation of
   \cref{lem:reduced-gain-variational-separation}.  For a subset
   \(U\subseteq S\) and a scalar \(\beta\in(0,1]\), define the one-layer
   signed coverage surplus
   \[
   \begin{aligned}
      \Omega_E(a;U,\beta)
      &=
      \sum_q
      \int
      h_{q,a}(\omega)
      \left[
         \beta\sum_{x\in U}W^E_{x,q}(\omega)
         -|U|\theta_q
      \right]d\nu_q(\omega)\\
      &\qquad - |U|\gamma .
   \end{aligned}
      \tag{LC1}
   \]
   Then a label \(a\) has positive reduced gain if and only if there are
   \(U\subseteq S\) and \(\beta\in(0,1]\) with
   \[
      \Omega_E(a;U,\beta)>0 .
      \tag{LC2}
   \]

   More precisely, for any intensity profile \(s\), let
   \(U_t=\{x:\ s_x>t\}\).  Then
   \[
      \Psi_E(a;s)
      =
      \int_0^\infty
      \Omega_E(a;U_t,e^{-t})\,dt .
      \tag{LC3}
   \]
   Conversely, if \(\Omega_E(a;U,e^{-t_0})>0\), then the uniform profile
   \(s_x=t_0\mathbf 1\{x\in U\}\), or an arbitrarily small positive multiple of
   \(\mathbf 1_U\) when \(t_0=0\), has positive surplus.

   Consequently, if a positive-gain label has a witnessing profile with
   \(0\le s_x\le B\), then it has a positive one-layer witness with
   \(\beta\ge e^{-B}\).  In that \(B\)-truncated case it is enough to test the
   dyadic grid
   \[
      \beta_j=2^{-j},
      \qquad
      0\le j\le\lceil B/\log 2\rceil,
      \tag{LC4}
   \]
   because increasing \(\beta\) can only increase \(\Omega_E(a;U,\beta)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   The layer-cake identities
   \[
      s_x=\int_0^\infty \mathbf 1\{s_x>t\}\,dt,
      \qquad
      1-e^{-s_x}=\int_0^\infty e^{-t}\mathbf 1\{s_x>t\}\,dt
   \]
   turn \((VG1)\) into \((LC3)\) after exchanging the finite sums and
   integrable layer terms.  If \(\Psi_E(a;s)>0\), then \((LC3)\) implies that
   \(\Omega_E(a;U_t,e^{-t})>0\) for some \(t\), proving one direction of
   \((LC2)\).

   Conversely, assume \(\Omega_E(a;U,e^{-t_0})>0\).  For every \(0\le t<t_0\),
   monotonicity in the first argument gives
   \[
      \Omega_E(a;U,e^{-t})
      \ge
      \Omega_E(a;U,e^{-t_0})>0 .
   \]
   Hence the profile \(s_x=t_0\mathbf 1\{x\in U\}\) has positive surplus by
   \((LC3)\) when \(t_0>0\).  If \(t_0=0\), the same strict inequality persists
   on a short interval \([0,\varepsilon]\), so
   \(s_x=\varepsilon\mathbf 1\{x\in U\}\) has positive surplus for sufficiently
   small \(\varepsilon>0\).  By
   \cref{lem:reduced-gain-variational-separation}, positive surplus is
   equivalent to positive reduced gain.

   If \(s_x\le B\), then any layer exposed by \((LC3)\) has
   \(t\le B\), hence \(\beta=e^{-t}\ge e^{-B}\).  Choose \(j\) with
   \(\beta\le 2^{-j}\le 2\beta\); this \(j\) lies in the range \((LC4)\).
   Since \(\Omega_E(a;U,\beta)\) is nondecreasing in \(\beta\), the dyadic
   value \(2^{-j}\) is also a positive witness.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Pointwise reduced-cost separation]
   \label{cor:pointwise-reduced-cost-separation}
   Work in the notation of
   \cref{lem:reduced-gain-variational-separation}.  For a candidate label
   \(a\), the following are equivalent:
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)>0,
      \tag{PW1}
   \]
   \[
      \exists x\in S:\quad G_x^E(a)>c_a,
      \tag{PW2}
   \]
   and
   \[
      \exists x\in S:\quad
      \sum_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)W^E_{x,q}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega)
      >
      \gamma+\sum_q\theta_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega).
      \tag{PW3}
   \]
   Equivalently, a reduced-gain separator may search over point-label pairs
   with the signed point score
   \[
      \operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)
      =
      \sum_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)W^E_{x,q}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega)
      -
      \gamma-\sum_q\theta_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega).
      \tag{PW4}
   \]
   Returning any pair with \(\operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)>0\) is a valid
   improving column.  Conversely, if
   \[
      \operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)\le0
      \qquad(x\in S,\ a\in\mathcal U)
      \tag{PW5}
   \]
   for an implicit label universe \(\mathcal U\), then the no-positive-gain
   condition \((IG3)\) of \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation} holds on
   \(\mathcal U\), and the current dual obstruction rules out every
   finite-support extension inside that universe.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
   By definition,
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)=\sum_x\Phi_{c_a}(G_x^E(a)).
   \]
   The scalar formula \((BG2)\) gives
   \(\Phi_c(G)>0\) exactly when \(G>c\), including the case \(c=0\).  Therefore
   the sum is positive exactly when at least one point satisfies
   \(G_x^E(a)>c_a\), proving \((PW1)\iff(PW2)\).  Expanding the definitions of
   \(G_x^E(a)\) and \(c_a\) gives \((PW2)\iff(PW3)\), and \((PW4)\) is the same
   inequality written as a score.

   A pair with positive score gives positive gain, so it is an improving column
   by \cref{cor:whole-bucket-reduced-gain}.  If \((PW5)\) holds, then
   \(G_x^E(a)\le c_a\) for every \(x\) and \(a\), hence every
   \(\Phi_{c_a}(G_x^E(a))\) vanishes.  Thus
   \(\operatorname{Gain}_E(a)=0\) for every \(a\in\mathcal U\), which is
   exactly \((IG3)\).  The final claim is
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite rate-coverage column generation]
   \label{lem:finite-rate-coverage-column-generation}
   Fix a finite candidate label family \(\mathcal B\) and a reporter
   \(A_q(\omega)\subseteq\mathcal B\).  Let
   \(E\subseteq S\times\mathcal B\) be an active set of point-label variables,
   and consider the restricted version of \((FC1)\)--\((FC3)\) in which
   \(b_{x,a}\) is allowed to be nonzero only for \((x,a)\in E\).  Suppose this
   restricted system is infeasible and has a dual certificate
   \((\lambda,\theta,\gamma)\) as in \((FC4)\).  Assume the corresponding
   restricted Lagrangian infimum is attained at \(b^E\).

   For any candidate pair \((x,a)\in S\times\mathcal B\), define
   \[
      c_a=\gamma+\sum_q\theta_q\Pr_{\nu_q}[a\in A_q(\omega)]
      \tag{GC0}
   \]
   and
   \[
      G_x^E(a)
      =
      \sum_{q:\,(x,q)\in\mathcal N}
      \lambda_{x,q}
      \int
      r_{x,q}(\omega)\mathbf 1\{a\in A_q(\omega)\}
      \exp\!\left(
         -\sum_{a'\in A_q(\omega)}b^E_{x,a'}
      \right)d\nu_q(\omega),
      \tag{GC1}
   \]
   with \(b^E_{x,a'}=0\) outside \(E\).  If
   \[
      G_x^E(a)\le c_a
      \qquad\text{for every inactive }(x,a)\notin E,
      \tag{GC2}
   \]
   then the same dual certificate proves infeasibility of the full convex system
   over all variables \(S\times\mathcal B\).

   Consequently, if the full candidate system over \(\mathcal B\) is feasible,
   then every infeasible restricted solve with an attained dual minimizer has an
   inactive pair \((x,a)\notin E\) with
   \[
      G_x^E(a)>c_a .
      \tag{GC3}
   \]
   Adding such a pair is a valid column-generation step.  Since
   \(S\times\mathcal B\) is finite, exhaustive column generation reaches a
   feasible restricted certificate whenever the full finite candidate family is
   feasible.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   Let \(\mathcal L_E\) be the dual Lagrangian in \((FC4)\), restricted to the
   active variables.  Since \(b^E\) attains its infimum on the nonnegative active
   orthant, the directional derivative in every active coordinate is
   nonnegative when the coordinate is zero and is zero when the coordinate is
   positive.  In the notation above, this is
   \[
      G_x^E(a)\le c_a,\qquad
      b^E_{x,a}\bigl(c_a-G_x^E(a)\bigr)=0
      \qquad((x,a)\in E).
      \tag{GC4}
   \]
   These are the KKT conditions for the convex differentiable Lagrangian on the
   active orthant.

   If \((GC2)\) also holds for inactive variables, then \(b^E\), extended by
   zero outside \(E\), satisfies the KKT conditions for the full nonnegative
   orthant \(S\times\mathcal B\).  Convexity therefore makes it a global
   minimizer of the full Lagrangian.  The restricted dual certificate has
   \[
      \mathcal L_E(b^E)>
      \sum_{(p,q)}\lambda_{p,q}\tau_{p,q}
      +\sum_q\theta_q\ell_q+\gamma S_0 ,
   \]
   and the full Lagrangian has the same value at the zero-extended \(b^E\).
   Hence the full infimum is also larger than the weighted target, so
   \((FC4)\) holds for the full system.  This proves full infeasibility.

   The contrapositive gives \((GC3)\) whenever the full candidate system is
   feasible.  Adding a positive-gain inactive pair strictly enlarges \(E\).
   There are only \(|S|\,|\mathcal B|\) pairs, so exhaustive repetition either
   reaches a feasible restricted system or exhausts all pairs.  The latter would
   contradict feasibility of the full system by the first part.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Seeded reduced-gain search]
   \label{lem:seeded-reduced-gain-search}
   Work in the finite candidate setting of
   \cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-column-generation}.  At an infeasible
   restricted solve with attained minimizer \(b^E\) and dual multipliers
   \((\lambda,\theta,\gamma)\), define \(c_a\), \(G_x^E(a)\), and
   \(\Phi_c\) by \((GC0)\), \((GC1)\), and \((BG2)\).  For a candidate label
   \(a\in\mathcal B\), put
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)
      =
      \sum_{x\in S}\Phi_{c_a}\!\left(G_x^E(a)\right).
      \tag{SG1}
   \]
   Let \(\mathsf P_E\) be any possibly randomized preprocessing proposal
   routine that outputs labels in \(\mathcal B\).  Suppose that, whenever the
   full finite candidate system over \(\mathcal B\) is feasible but the current
   restricted system is infeasible,
   \[
      \Pr_{a\sim\mathsf P_E}\!\left[
         \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)>0
      \right]\ge p_E .
      \tag{SG2}
   \]
   Then exact verification of proposed labels and Las Vegas repetition finds a
   positive-gain label after expected at most \(1/p_E\) proposals.  Adding all
   inactive pairs
   \[
      (x,a)\notin E
      \qquad\text{with}\qquad
      G_x^E(a)>c_a
      \tag{SG3}
   \]
   is a valid column-generation augmentation.

   Consequently, if \(p_E\ge p_0>0\) at every infeasible restricted solve, the
   seeded column-generation algorithm reaches a feasible active certificate in
   expected at most
   \[
      |S|\,|\mathcal B|/p_0
   \]
   proposal calls.  More generally, the same conclusion holds with total
   expected proposal count bounded by the sum of \(1/p_E\) over the
   augmentations that occur.  If instead one runs
   \(R=\lceil p_E^{-1}\log(1/\delta)\rceil\) independent proposals at a fixed
   restricted solve, a positive-gain label is found with probability at least
   \(1-\delta\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   By \cref{cor:whole-bucket-reduced-gain}, \(\operatorname{Gain}_E(a)>0\)
   is equivalent to the existence of at least one point \(x\) with
   \(G_x^E(a)>c_a\), and the simultaneous assignment \(s_x\) in \((BG3)\)
   strictly decreases the dual Lagrangian.  Thus every pair in \((SG3)\) is an
   improving inactive column in the sense of
   \cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-column-generation}.

   Exact verification computes \(\operatorname{Gain}_E(a)\) for each proposed
   label and accepts precisely when it is positive.  Under \((SG2)\), the
   number of proposals until acceptance is stochastically dominated by a
   geometric random variable of mean \(1/p_E\), and \(R\) independent proposals
   fail with probability at most \((1-p_E)^R\le\delta\).

   Each successful augmentation adds at least one previously inactive pair
   \((x,a)\) and never removes active pairs.  There are only
   \(|S|\,|\mathcal B|\) such pairs.  If the full candidate system is feasible,
   \cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-column-generation} guarantees that an
   infeasible restricted solve always has a positive-gain inactive pair; the
   exposure hypothesis \((SG2)\) lets the seeded proposal routine find one with
   the displayed expected effort.  Summing the geometric waiting times over the
   finitely many augmentations proves the expected proposal bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Implicit reduced-gain separation]
   \label{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}
   Let \(\mathcal U\) be an arbitrary implicit label universe.  For each
   \(a\in\mathcal U\) and query \(q\), let
   \(h_{q,a}(\omega)=\mathbf 1\{a\in A_q(\omega)\}\) be a measurable reporter
   event.  Let \(E\subseteq S\times\mathcal U\) be finite, and consider the
   restricted rate-coverage system in which only variables in \(E\) may be
   nonzero.  Suppose this restricted system is infeasible, has a dual
   certificate \((\lambda,\theta,\gamma)\), and has an attained restricted
   dual minimizer \(b^E\).

   For every \(a\in\mathcal U\), define
   \[
      c_a=\gamma+\sum_q\theta_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)\,d\nu_q(\omega),
      \tag{IG0}
   \]
   \[
      G_x^E(a)
      =
      \sum_{q:\,(x,q)\in\mathcal N}
      \lambda_{x,q}
      \int
      r_{x,q}(\omega)h_{q,a}(\omega)
      \exp\!\left(
         -\sum_{(x,a')\in E}
         h_{q,a'}(\omega)b^E_{x,a'}
      \right)d\nu_q(\omega),
      \tag{IG1}
   \]
   and
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)
      =
      \sum_{x\in S}\Phi_{c_a}\!\left(G_x^E(a)\right).
      \tag{IG2}
   \]
   If
   \[
      \operatorname{Gain}_E(a)=0
      \qquad(a\in\mathcal U),
      \tag{IG3}
   \]
   then the same dual certificate proves infeasibility of every finite-support
   extension of \(E\) inside \(S\times\mathcal U\).  Equivalently, no feasible
   rate-coverage certificate with finite label support exists in
   \(\mathcal U\).

   Thus an exact reduced-gain separator for \(\mathcal U\), which either
   returns a label \(a\) with \(\operatorname{Gain}_E(a)>0\) or certifies
   \((IG3)\), is a complete build-time separation oracle for finite-support
   certificates.  If the finite-support system over \(\mathcal U\) is feasible
   and the current restricted system is infeasible, the separator must return a
   positive-gain label.

   Moreover, suppose labels are quotient by the equivalence relation
   \(a\sim a'\) when
   \(h_{q,a}=h_{q,a'}\) \(\nu_q\)-almost everywhere for every \(q\), and the
   quotient has \(N\) classes.  If column generation keeps at most one
   representative of each quotient class and adds all pairs
   \((x,a)\) with \(G_x^E(a)>c_a\) for every positive-gain representative it
   receives, then an exact separator over quotient representatives either
   produces a feasible active certificate or proves finite-support
   infeasibility after at most \(|S|N\) successful augmentations.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
   The attained minimizer \(b^E\) satisfies the active KKT conditions exactly
   as in the proof of \cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-column-generation}:
   \[
      G_x^E(a)\le c_a,\qquad
      b^E_{x,a}\bigl(c_a-G_x^E(a)\bigr)=0
      \qquad((x,a)\in E).
      \tag{IG4}
   \]
   Since \(\Phi_c(G)=0\) is equivalent to \(G\le c\), condition \((IG3)\)
   implies \(G_x^E(a)\le c_a\) for every inactive pair
   \((x,a)\notin E\).  Hence \(b^E\), extended by zero, satisfies the KKT
   conditions on \(E\cup F\) for every finite set
   \(F\subseteq S\times\mathcal U\).  Convexity of the finite-dimensional
   Lagrangian on that active orthant makes this extension a global minimizer
   over \(E\cup F\).

   The restricted dual certificate has Lagrangian value strictly larger than
   the weighted target in \((FC4)\).  The zero-extended point has the same value
   on every finite extension \(E\cup F\), and it is the minimizer there.  Thus
   the same multipliers satisfy \((FC4)\) for every finite extension.  Every
   finite-support rate-coverage certificate uses some such finite set \(F\), so
   no finite-support feasible certificate can exist.

   The separator statement is the contrapositive: if a finite-support feasible
   certificate exists and the current restricted system is infeasible, then
   \((IG3)\) cannot hold, so some label has positive gain and an exact separator
   must return one.  The quotient statement follows because all quantities
   \((IG0)\)--\((IG2)\), and all primal constraints involving a label, depend
   only on the reporter events \(h_{q,a}\) up to \(\nu_q\)-null sets.  With one
   representative per quotient class, each successful augmentation adds at
   least one previously inactive pair in the finite set of size \(|S|N\).
   If the process exhausts those pairs without feasibility, the no-positive-gain
   certificate above proves finite-support infeasibility over the entire
   quotient universe.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Soft list-decoding reduced-cost separator]
   \label{cor:soft-list-decoding-reduced-cost-separator}
   Let \(\mathcal U\subseteq\prod_{i=1}^h\Sigma_i\) be an implicit code,
   trellis, or residue label universe.  Fix a restricted dual state \(E\) as in
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}.  Suppose that, for every
   \(x\in S\), the signed point score from
   \cref{cor:pointwise-reduced-cost-separation} has the coordinatewise form
   \[
      \operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)
      =
      \kappa_E(x)+\sum_{i=1}^h\psi_{E,x,i}(a_i),
      \qquad a=(a_1,\ldots,a_h)\in\mathcal U,
      \tag{SL1}
   \]
   where \(\kappa_E(x)\) and the reliabilities
   \(\psi_{E,x,i}:\Sigma_i\to\mathbb R\) are computable during
   preprocessing.

   Assume a certified soft threshold decoder for \(\mathcal U\): on input
   reliabilities \((\psi_i)_{i=1}^h\) and threshold \(\Theta\), it returns a
   finite list \(\mathsf L(\psi,\Theta)\) containing every label satisfying
   \[
      \sum_{i=1}^h\psi_i(a_i)>\Theta.
      \tag{SL2}
   \]
   Returned labels may be a superset, provided each returned label can be scored
   exactly and filtered.

   Then the following procedure is an exact pointwise reduced-cost separator
   over \(\mathcal U\).  For each \(x\in S\), call the decoder with
   \[
      \psi_i=\psi_{E,x,i},
      \qquad
      \Theta=-\kappa_E(x),
      \tag{SL3}
   \]
   score the returned labels exactly, and return any pair \((x,a)\) with
   \(\operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)>0\).  If no such pair is found for any
   \(x\), then
   \[
      \operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)\le0
      \qquad(x\in S,\ a\in\mathcal U),
      \tag{SL4}
   \]
   so the no-positive-gain certificate \((IG3)\) holds on \(\mathcal U\).
   Consequently, this decoder is a complete build-time separation oracle for
   finite-support rate-coverage certificates inside \(\mathcal U\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
   If a positive pair exists, then for that point \(x\)
   \[
      \sum_{i=1}^h\psi_{E,x,i}(a_i)>-\kappa_E(x).
   \]
   The decoder call \((SL3)\) must therefore include \(a\) in its output list.
   Exact filtering accepts it, so the procedure finds a positive pair.
   Conversely, if the procedure finds no positive pair, then no label can have
   satisfied the strict threshold for any \(x\), proving \((SL4)\).  The
   equivalence between \((SL4)\), \((IG3)\), and finite-support infeasibility
   over the implicit universe is exactly
   \cref{cor:pointwise-reduced-cost-separation} and
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Threshold-event reduced-cost normal form]
   \label{cor:threshold-event-reduced-cost}
   Work in the finite-outcome version of
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}: each \(\nu_q\) is supported on
   a finite set \(\Omega_q\).  Let
   \(\mathcal U\subseteq\prod_{i=1}^h\Sigma_i\), and suppose every reporter
   event is a coordinatewise soft threshold,
   \[
      h_{q,a}(\omega)
      =
      \mathbf 1\left\{
      \kappa_{q,\omega}+\sum_{i=1}^h\phi_{q,\omega,i}(a_i)>0
      \right\}.
      \tag{TE1}
   \]
   For a restricted dual state \(E\) and a point \(x\), define the signed
   threshold-test weights
   \[
      \beta^E_{x,q,\omega}
      =
      \nu_q(\omega)\bigl(W^E_{x,q}(\omega)-\theta_q\bigr),
      \tag{TE2}
   \]
   where \(\nu_q(\omega)=\nu_q\{\omega\}\).  Then the pointwise reduced-cost
   score of \cref{cor:pointwise-reduced-cost-separation} is exactly
   \[
   \begin{aligned}
      \operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)
      &=
      -\gamma+
      \sum_q\sum_{\omega\in\Omega_q}
      \beta^E_{x,q,\omega}
      \mathbf 1\left\{
      \kappa_{q,\omega}
      +\sum_{i=1}^h\phi_{q,\omega,i}(a_i)>0
      \right\}.
   \end{aligned}
      \tag{TE3}
   \]

   Consequently, a certified signed threshold-coverage decoder for
   \(\mathcal U\) is a complete reduced-cost separator: on input finite tests
   \((\kappa_t,\phi_{t,i})\), signed weights \((\beta_t)\), and a threshold
   \(\Gamma\), it either returns \(a\in\mathcal U\) with
   \[
      \sum_t\beta_t
      \mathbf 1\left\{
      \kappa_t+\sum_i\phi_{t,i}(a_i)>0
      \right\}>\Gamma
      \tag{TE4}
   \]
   or certifies that no such label exists.  Running this decoder for each
   \(x\in S\), with \(\Gamma=\gamma\) and weights \((TE2)\), either returns a
   positive reduced-cost point-label pair or certifies \((IG3)\) over
   \(\mathcal U\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
   Substitute \((TE1)\) into the point score
   \[
      \sum_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)W^E_{x,q}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega)
      -
      \gamma-\sum_q\theta_q\int h_{q,a}(\omega)d\nu_q(\omega).
   \]
   Since the outcome laws are finite, the integrals are sums, and the
   coefficient of each threshold event is precisely \((TE2)\).  This gives
   \((TE3)\).

   A label satisfying \((TE4)\) with \(\Gamma=\gamma\) has
   \(\operatorname{Score}_E(x,a)>0\), hence is an improving pair by
   \cref{cor:pointwise-reduced-cost-separation}.  If the decoder certifies that
   no such label exists for every \(x\), then all point scores are nonpositive;
   \cref{cor:pointwise-reduced-cost-separation} gives \((IG3)\) and
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation} gives finite-support
   infeasibility over \(\mathcal U\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Positive-coverage screening for reduced cost]
   \label{cor:positive-coverage-screen}
   In the setting of \cref{cor:threshold-event-reduced-cost}, write the signed
   test weights as
   \[
      \beta_t=\beta_t^+-\beta_t^-,
      \qquad
      \beta_t^+=\max\{\beta_t,0\},\quad
      \beta_t^-=\max\{-\beta_t,0\}.
      \tag{PS1}
   \]
   For a label \(a\), let
   \[
      P(a)=
      \sum_t\beta_t^+
      \mathbf 1\left\{
      \kappa_t+\sum_i\phi_{t,i}(a_i)>0
      \right\}
      \tag{PS2}
   \]
   be its positive coverage, and let \(S(a)\) be the signed coverage in
   \((TE4)\).  Since \(S(a)\le P(a)\), every reduced-cost witness with
   \(S(a)>\Gamma\) also satisfies
   \[
      P(a)>\Gamma .
      \tag{PS3}
   \]

   Consequently, an exact nonnegative threshold-coverage lister is enough for
   signed reduced-cost separation.  Namely, suppose that on input nonnegative
   weights \((w_t)\), tests \((\kappa_t,\phi_{t,i})\), and threshold
   \(\Gamma\), the lister returns a finite superset of all labels with
   \[
      \sum_t w_t
      \mathbf 1\left\{
      \kappa_t+\sum_i\phi_{t,i}(a_i)>0
      \right\}>\Gamma .
      \tag{PS4}
   \]
   Run it with \(w_t=\beta_t^+\), score every returned label by the original
   signed objective \(S(a)\), and return any label with \(S(a)>\Gamma\).  If no
   returned label passes this exact filter, then no label in \(\mathcal U\)
   has positive signed reduced cost.

   Thus the code/lattice offline oracle may be stated as a one-sided positive
   coverage lister plus exact verification of signed reduced cost; negative
   tests never have to be used to generate candidates.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
   The inequality \(S(a)\le P(a)\) follows immediately from
   \[
      S(a)=P(a)-
      \sum_t\beta_t^-
      \mathbf 1\left\{
      \kappa_t+\sum_i\phi_{t,i}(a_i)>0
      \right\}.
   \]
   Hence any label with \(S(a)>\Gamma\) is included in the lister output for
   the nonnegative weights \(w_t=\beta_t^+\).  Exact filtering accepts every
   true signed witness and rejects every false candidate.  If the filter finds
   none, then no signed witness exists, so
   \cref{cor:threshold-event-reduced-cost} certifies the corresponding
   no-positive reduced-cost condition.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Dyadic unweighting for positive list recovery]
   \label{cor:dyadic-positive-list-recovery}
   In the positive-coverage lister of \cref{cor:positive-coverage-screen}, let
   \(E_t(a)\) denote the Boolean threshold event in \((PS2)\), let
   \(w_t\ge0\), and let \(\Gamma\ge0\).  Partition the positive weights into
   nonempty dyadic classes \(T_j\), with representatives \(\lambda_j>0\),
   such that
   \[
      t\in T_j
      \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
      \lambda_j\le w_t<2\lambda_j .
      \tag{DU1}
   \]
   Write \(B\) for the number of nonempty classes and
   \[
      N_j(a)=\sum_{t\in T_j}E_t(a).
      \tag{DU2}
   \]
   If \(B=0\), then no label has positive coverage above \(\Gamma\).  Otherwise,
   suppose that for each \(j\) an unweighted coverage lister returns a finite
   superset of all labels satisfying
   \[
      N_j(a)>
      \frac{\Gamma}{2B\lambda_j}.
      \tag{DU3}
   \]
   Then the union of these \(B\) lists contains every label with
   \[
      \sum_t w_tE_t(a)>\Gamma .
      \tag{DU4}
   \]
   Exact evaluation of the weighted positive coverage on the returned labels
   therefore gives a complete positive-coverage lister.

   Thus arbitrary nonnegative dual weights can be moved out of the decoder and
   into a logarithmic number of unweighted list-recovery calls, followed by the
   same exact reduced-cost verification.  In the ordinary list-recovery special
   case, \(E_t(a)\) is a coordinate or block membership test.  For a lattice or
   trellis quotient, \(E_t\) is the corresponding local residue or path test;
   the same statement applies to the implicit coverage code
   \(a\mapsto(E_t(a))_t\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
   The case \(B=0\) is immediate because all weights are zero.  Assume
   \(B>0\), and let \(P(a)=\sum_t w_tE_t(a)\).  If a label \(a\) is not
   returned by any unweighted lister, then \(N_j(a)\le
   \Gamma/(2B\lambda_j)\) for every \(j\).  By \((DU1)\),
   \[
      \sum_{t\in T_j}w_tE_t(a)
      \le
      2\lambda_j N_j(a)
      \le
      \frac{\Gamma}{B}.
   \]
   Summing over the \(B\) classes gives \(P(a)\le\Gamma\).  Hence every label
   with \(P(a)>\Gamma\) appears in the union of the unweighted lists.  Exact
   filtering by \(P(a)>\Gamma\) removes all false positives.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Unified soft-decoded rate-coverage compiler]
   \label{cor:unified-soft-decoded-rate-coverage}
   Let \(\mathcal U\subseteq\prod_{i=1}^h\Sigma_i\) be a finite quotient label
   family represented by a certified soft threshold decoder.  Assume the online
   reporter has the coordinatewise form
   \[
      A_q(\omega)
      =
      \left\{
      a\in\mathcal U:\
      \kappa^{\rm rep}_{q,\omega}
      +\sum_{i=1}^h\phi^{\rm rep}_{q,\omega,i}(a_i)>0
      \right\},
      \tag{US1}
   \]
   and that the decoder returns a certified superset
   \(\widehat A_q(\omega)\supseteq A_q(\omega)\), from which \(A_q(\omega)\)
   is obtained by exact filtering, in work
   \[
      D^{\rm rep}(q,\omega)+O(|\widehat A_q(\omega)|).
      \tag{US2}
   \]
   Also assume that preprocessing has an exact reduced-cost separator for every
   restricted dual state arising during column generation: it either returns a
   positive reduced-cost point-label pair or certifies \((IG3)\) over
   \(\mathcal U\).  This separator may be implemented by the additive
   soft-decoding interface of
   \cref{cor:soft-list-decoding-reduced-cost-separator}, by the signed
   threshold-coverage interface of \cref{cor:threshold-event-reduced-cost}, or
   by the positive-coverage screen of
   \cref{cor:positive-coverage-screen}, by the dyadic unweighted
   list-recovery screen of \cref{cor:dyadic-positive-list-recovery}, or by any
   other exact offline optimization over the implicit quotient family.
   Its running time is a preprocessing cost, not a query resource.

   Suppose the finite rate-coverage system over \(S\times\mathcal U\) is
   feasible with targets \((\tau_{p,q})\), \((\ell_q)\), and \(S_0\), and with
   attained restricted dual minimizers whenever the restricted solve is
   infeasible.  Then arbitrary preprocessing can find a feasible finite active
   intensity certificate \(b\) using decoder calls rather than retaining an
   explicit list of all labels: start from any finite active set, solve the
   restricted convex problem, and, on infeasibility, run the soft reduced-cost
   separator and add the returned positive pair.  The process terminates after at most
   \[
      |S|\,|\mathcal U/{\sim}|
      \tag{US3}
   \]
   successful pair additions, where \(\sim\) is equality of reporter events.

   The retained index consists only of the nonzero point-label intensities and
   the compact decoder for \(\mathcal U\).  For a query \(q\), its expected
   online work is bounded by
   \[
      \E_{\omega\sim\nu_q}D^{\rm rep}(q,\omega)
      +
      \E_{\omega\sim\nu_q}|\widehat A_q(\omega)|
      +
      \ell_q ,
      \tag{US4}
   \]
   where the last term is the expected reported bucket-incidence scan.  Its
   expected stored incidence count is \(S_0\).  In particular, if
   \[
      S_0\le m^{1+\rho}\log^{O_c(1)}m
      \tag{US5}
   \]
   and the right side of \((US4)\) is
   \(D\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m\) for every promised query, then the resulting
   retained panel satisfies the implementation requirements of
   \cref{asm:rate-coverage-panel}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
   The online reporter is implemented by the threshold decoder through
   \((US1)\)--\((US2)\), followed by exact filtering if the decoder returns a
   certified superset.  During preprocessing, if a restricted convex solve is
   infeasible, the exact reduced-cost separator either returns a positive
   pair or proves \((IG3)\) over \(\mathcal U\).  The latter is impossible
   while the full finite system over \(\mathcal U\) is feasible, by
   \cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation}.  Thus every infeasible
   restricted solve yields a valid new pair.  Since the quotient family has
   only \(|\mathcal U/{\sim}|\) reporter classes, at most \((US3)\) distinct
   point-label pairs can be added before either feasibility is reached or the
   quotient is exhausted.  Exhaustion would contradict full feasibility by the
   same implicit-separation lemma.

   Once a feasible intensity vector \(b\) is found, the Poisson materialization
   of \cref{cor:rate-coverage-intensity} stores expected total incidence
   \(\sum_{x,a}b_{x,a}\le S_0\).  For a query outcome \(\omega\), the decoder
   cost is the first two terms of \((US4)\).  The expected number of candidate
   incidences scanned from exactly filtered reported nonempty buckets is
   \[
      \int
      \sum_{x\in S}\sum_{a\in A_q(\omega)}b_{x,a}\,d\nu_q(\omega)
      =
      L_q(b)
      \le \ell_q .
   \]
   The miss constraints are exactly the finite constraints \((FC1)\).  Hence
   \((US5)\) and the displayed query-work bound give the storage and reporter
   requirements in \cref{asm:rate-coverage-panel}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Offline certificate for facility search]
   \label{rem:rate-coverage-offline-certificate}
   \Cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-convex} is the build-time certificate
   suggested by the practical \(k\)-means++ and multi-assignment experiments.
   A proposed label reporter supplies the sets \(A_q(\omega)\).  Expensive
   preprocessing then solves the convex intensity problem over \(b_{x,a}\).
   Passing the primal inequalities gives the retained replication profile.
   Failing them gives dual prices \(\lambda,\theta,\gamma\): near witnesses
   whose tilted miss is expensive, queries whose reported load is expensive,
   and a global storage price.  These prices can drive column generation over
   new centers, \(k\)-means++ seeds, product-code labels, or lattice quotient
   buckets.  \Cref{lem:rate-coverage-column-gain} gives the actual score to
   optimize: a proposed label is valuable for point \(x\) when its residual
   tilted witness coverage \(G_x(a)\) exceeds its dual query-load and storage
   price, and \cref{cor:whole-bucket-reduced-gain} sums the optimal gains over
   all points assigned to that bucket.  \Cref{lem:finite-rate-coverage-column-generation}
   turns this into an exact finite build algorithm: if a finite candidate family
   is feasible, then every infeasible restricted solve reveals such a
   positive-gain pair until a feasible active certificate is found.  Thus the
   theorem target is no longer Lloyd convergence; it is the absence of a
   positive dual obstruction for a compact decodable label family.
   \Cref{lem:reduced-gain-variational-separation} rewrites that obstruction as
   a signed weighted coverage objective after choosing candidate intensities,
   so a code, lattice, trellis, or facility generator can search with its native
   weighted reporter and then verify the exact nonlinear gain.
   \Cref{lem:layer-cake-reduced-gain-separation} reduces this further to one
   uniform intensity layer on a point subset; under a preprocessing-certified
   intensity cap, only a logarithmic dyadic grid of layer weights has to be
   tested.  For pure separation, \cref{cor:pointwise-reduced-cost-separation}
   collapses even that to a single point-label score; whole-bucket gain remains
   a greedy strength objective, not a separate completeness requirement.
   \Cref{lem:seeded-reduced-gain-search} is the build-time version of the same
   statement.  A \(k\)-means++ sampler, repulsive facility search, product-code
   threshold reporter, Construction-A residue decoder, or lattice
   discrete-Gaussian sampler may be used as the proposal routine
   \(\mathsf P_E\).  The only property needed for the proof is exposure of a
   positive reduced-gain label against every surviving dual obstruction; the
   proposed label is then verified by the exact finite convex certificate
   before it is retained.  \Cref{lem:implicit-reduced-gain-separation} removes
   the need to enumerate the proposal universe in advance.  A retained
   code/lattice/trellis decoder may act as an implicit separator over its
   quotient label classes: either it exposes a positive reduced-gain bucket, or
   the current dual obstruction certifies that no finite-support panel from that
   universe can solve the rate-coverage constraints.
   \Cref{cor:soft-list-decoding-reduced-cost-separator} is the concrete
   code/lattice version of this interface.  If the reduced point score separates
   into coordinate reliabilities, then a certified soft list decoder, stack
   decoder, or residue-code list-recovery oracle is already an exact
   reduced-cost separator after exact filtering.  For the more common case
   where reporter events are themselves coordinatewise threshold predicates,
   \cref{cor:threshold-event-reduced-cost} identifies the exact offline
   objective as signed threshold coverage: maximize the dual-priced sum of
   satisfied reporter tests.  \Cref{cor:positive-coverage-screen} simplifies
   even this: enumerate labels with enough positively priced reporter tests and
   use the negative tests only in the exact verifier.
   \Cref{cor:dyadic-positive-list-recovery} removes one more mismatch with the
   coding literature: real dual weights can be grouped into dyadic classes, so
   the proposal routine only has to solve unweighted agreement/list-recovery
   instances and the exact verifier restores the original weighted objective.
   This is the transferable content of soft-decision Reed--Solomon decoding
   \cite{KV03}, high-rate expander and tensor-code list recovery
   \cite{HW15,HRW17,ST25}, Construction-A quotient decoding, and
   Barnes--Wall style lattice recursion \cite{GP17,MP20}: the decoder optimizes
   or enumerates a dual-priced score/coverage surrogate, while the convex
   certificate verifies storage, query load, and tilted witness coverage before
   retaining the bucket.
   \Cref{cor:unified-soft-decoded-rate-coverage} composes the
   pieces into the actual build contract: the query-facing decoder implements
   online reporter enumeration, any exact offline separator may drive column
   generation during preprocessing, and the final retained intensities are
   accepted only by the rate-coverage verifier.
\end{remark}

\begin{assumption}[Compact rate-coverage panel]
   \label{asm:rate-coverage-panel}
   Fix \(c>1\) and \(\rho=\rho_c\).  For every bounded-diameter localized node
   with \(m\) resident points in projected dimension \(D\), preprocessing can
   construct a label reporter, point distributions \((\mu_x)_{x\in S}\), and
   replication budgets \((a_x)_{x\in S}\) satisfying \((RC1)\)--\((RC2)\), with
   \[
      \sum_{x\in S}a_x
      \le
      m^{1+\rho}\log^{O_c(1)}m .
      \tag{RC4}
   \]
   The reporter computes \(\mathcal A(q,Z)\) in expected time
   \(D\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m\), including duplicate removal up to the reported
   incidence bound.
\end{assumption}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional rate-coverage posterior endpoint]
   \label{prop:rate-coverage-polylog}
   Under \cref{asm:rate-coverage-panel}, worst-case Euclidean ANN has query
   time
   \[
      Q(n,d)=d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n
   \]
   and space
   \[
      S(n,d)=dn+n^{1+\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n .
   \]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
   In each localized node, materialize the Poisson buckets from
   \cref{lem:poisson-rate-coverage}.  The expected stored incidences are within
   the local space budget by \((RC4)\); the usual truncation-and-rebuild
   convention turns this into capped space at a constant-factor loss.  For a
   promised query and any fixed near witness \(p\), \((RC1)\) gives the
   tilted witness-loss condition in expectation over preprocessing.  Repeating
   the proof of \cref{lem:smoothed-witness-list-compiler} with preprocessing
   included in the outer expectation gives one-trial success
   \(\Omega_c(1/\log^{A_c}m)\).  The expected scan and reporting work per trial
   is \(D\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m\) by \((RC2)\) and the reporter guarantee.
   Polylogarithmically many independent query trials give constant success.
   Summing over the localized copies and verifying all returned candidates in
   the original metric gives the displayed global bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What \(k\)-means++ would have to prove]
   \label{rem:kmeanspp-facility-target}
   \Cref{cor:facility-bucket-witness} is the k-means-friendly version of the
   posterior route.  A Lloyd or \(k\)-means++ run \cite{AV07} may be useful at
   build time because it proposes data-adapted facility buckets, but the
   analysis does not need to certify convergence to a centroid optimum.  It
   needs the two inequalities \((FB1)\) and the expected bucket-union load.  In
   words, under the tilted Gaussian law that moves the query a distance
   \(\alpha\|p-q\|\) toward the hidden near point and adds noise
   \(I_D/t\), the reported facilities must contain \(p\) with constant
   weighted mass, while far and irrelevant buckets must not inflate the scan
   beyond \(m^\rho\polylog m\).

   \Cref{lem:poisson-rate-coverage} makes this quantitative.  Assigning point
   \(p\) to \(a_p\) independent labels from a distribution \(\mu_p\), while the
   query probes labels \(B=\mathcal A(q,Z)\), misses \(p\) with probability
   \(\exp(-a_p\mu_p(B))\).  Thus the right redundancy rule is a water-filling
   problem over the scalar coverages \(\mu_p(\mathcal A(q,Z))\) under the
   tilted witness law.  \Cref{lem:finite-signature-rate-coverage} explains how
   this can be certified for a continuous query domain: group queries by
   reporter signatures and prove finite upper envelopes for the tilted miss and
   load coefficients.  \Cref{cor:finite-transcript-rate-coverage} is the
   list-decoding form of the same certificate: finite decoder transcripts or
   lattice quotient states replace enumeration of all label subsets and charge
   their reporter work directly.  \Cref{cor:compressed-decoder-dag-rate-coverage}
   further quotients the transcript tree to a compact acyclic decoder state
   graph, which is the natural retained object for trellises, polar decoders,
   and lattice enumeration.  \Cref{cor:output-sensitive-decoder-dag-rate-coverage}
   covers the list-enumeration case: the miss certificate may charge a
   responsibility-weighted useful output, while scan work is charged to every
   reported bucket.  \Cref{lem:scalar-redundancy-waterfill} gives the exact
   marginal rule once those coefficients and assignment laws are fixed: keep
   adding redundancy to the points whose current marginal tilted coverage
   \(D_p(a_p)\) is above the storage price.  Fixed top-\(k\) multi-assignment,
   adaptive boundary replication, and \(k\)-means++ seeding are all proposal
   mechanisms for the same certificate: small tilted miss \((RC1)\), small
   reported load \((RC2)\), and total storage \((RC4)\).

   This also suggests a cleaner practical alternative to plain Lloyd cells:
   use expensive preprocessing to search over many soft or multi-assignment
   facility panels--for example squared-distance seeded panels, repulsive
   center sets, product-code panels, or lattice quotient buckets--and retain
   only panels whose bucket-load and tilted witness-capture certificates are
   good.  The theory-guided analogue of \(k\)-means++ is therefore not
   distance-squared seeding by itself, but reduced-gain seeding against the
   current convex dual obstruction: propose a label with large
   dual-priced tilted witness coverage per query-load and storage price, then
   accept it only after the exact finite verifier confirms positive gain.
   For a code, trellis, or lattice family, the same dual prices become soft
   reliabilities for the offline list-decoding or threshold-coverage separator.
   The retained query algorithm is then just a facility reporter plus verified
   bucket scanning, while the proof object is the pair of finite spectrum
   inequalities above.
\end{remark}

\begin{assumption}[Compact smoothed witness-list decoder]
   \label{asm:smoothed-witness-list}
   Fix \(c>1\) and \(\rho=\rho_c\).  For every bounded-diameter localized node
   with \(m\) resident points in projected dimension \(D\), there is a retained
   candidate-list decoder of space
   \[
      m^{1+\rho}\log^{O_c(1)}m
   \]
   such that, for every promised query \(q\) and every near witness
   \(p\in S\cap\Ball(q,r)\), the tilted witness-loss condition \((WL2)\) holds.
   One list can be generated and verified in expected query time
   \[
      D\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m .
   \]
\end{assumption}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional witness-list posterior endpoint]
   \label{prop:witness-list-polylog}
   Under \cref{asm:smoothed-witness-list}, worst-case Euclidean ANN has query
   time
   \[
      Q(n,d)=d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n
   \]
   and space
   \[
      S(n,d)=dn+n^{1+\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n .
   \]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
   Apply the projected bounded-diameter localization of
   \cref{lem:localization}.  In a good localized copy containing a true near
   pair \(p,q\), run \cref{lem:smoothed-witness-list-compiler} with the
   retained decoder from \cref{asm:smoothed-witness-list}.  The
   \(O_c(\log^{A_c}m)\) rejection repetitions contribute only a polylogarithmic
   factor, and the local expected query work is
   \(D\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m\).  Projection and final metric verification are
   absorbed in the displayed ambient \(d\)-dependent bound.  The space bound is
   the retained local decoder space plus raw coordinates, summed over the
   polylogarithmic localized copies.
\end{proof}

\begin{assumption}[Smoothed structured nearest-cell decoder]
   \label{asm:smoothed-nearest-cell}
   Fix \(c>1\) and \(\rho=\rho_c\).  For every bounded-diameter localized node
   with \(m\) resident points in projected dimension \(D\), there is a retained
   structured decoder of space
   \[
      m^{1+\rho}\log^{O_c(1)}m
   \]
   that, for every query point \(Z\in\mathbb R^D\), returns the exact nearest
   resident point \(N_S(Z)\) with the fixed tie-breaking in projected query
   time
   \[
      D\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m .
   \]
\end{assumption}

\begin{remark}[Nearest cell means structured decoding]
   \label{rem:nearest-cell-structured-decoding}
   \Cref{asm:smoothed-nearest-cell} is a normal form for the remaining
   posterior data-structure problem, not a consequence of ordinary exact or
   approximate nearest-neighbor search on an arbitrary resident set.  In an
   unstructured point set, exact Voronoi-cell decoding is precisely the hard
   exact nearest-neighbor problem, and a multiplicative ANN oracle is too weak
   for the pointwise domination needed by the rejection compiler.  The intended
   source of the primitive is a retained code, lattice, trellis, or quotient
   panel whose cells have a compact decoder.

   The list-decoding analogy is therefore literal.  The Gaussian perturbation
   \(Z\sim N(q,I_D/t)\) is the received word, the resident Voronoi cells are
   the decoded codeword regions, and \cref{cor:truncated-nearest-cell-compiler}
   only adds a scalar Fano-style reweighting.  Product-code filters in the
   sense of Becker--Ducas--Gama--Laarhoven, Barnes--Wall or Barnes--Sloane
   recursive decoders, Construction-A theta quotients, and soft list-recovery
   blocks are useful only insofar as they give this compact exact-cell or
   aggregate-cell sampler with the same storage and query certificates.  A
   Lloyd or \(k\)-means partition may be an excellent build-time proposal for
   such cells, but the proof object must be the certified decoder and its
   cell-spectrum bounds.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional nearest-cell posterior endpoint]
   \label{prop:nearest-cell-polylog}
   Under \cref{asm:smoothed-nearest-cell}, worst-case Euclidean ANN has query
   time
   \[
      Q(n,d)=d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n
   \]
   and space
   \[
      S(n,d)=dn+n^{1+\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n .
   \]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
   Apply the projected bounded-diameter localization of
   \cref{lem:localization}.  In a good localized copy containing a true near
   pair, choose the dimension-aware parameters \((QC0)\) and run
   \cref{cor:truncated-nearest-cell-compiler}.  The rejection cap
   \(B=\log^{A_c}m\) contributes only a polylogarithmic factor, so the local
   query work is
   \[
      D\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m .
   \]
   The ambient projection and final metric verification are absorbed in the
   displayed \(d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n\) global bound.  The compiler
   returns a valid point with constant probability in the good copy.  The
   retained nearest-cell structures and the raw coordinates fit the displayed
   local space bound, and summing over the polylogarithmic localized copies
   gives the global query and space bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the Voronoi form changes]
   \label{rem:alpha-leader-voronoi-capture}
   \Cref{lem:alpha-leader-voronoi-capture} removes the opaque maximum from the
   posterior sampler.  The remaining law is a distance-tilted Gaussian measure
   of exact nearest-neighbor cells:
   each candidate \(x\) is weighted by a kernel centered at the query and by
   the probability that a Gaussian perturbation of the interpolated point
   \(s_\alpha q+\alpha x\) falls in \(x\)'s Voronoi cell.  Thus
   \cref{asm:alpha-leader-sampler} can be attacked either as a compact
   sampler for these smoothed Voronoi cell masses, or through a lattice/code
   decoder that samples nearest-cell representatives under the same Gaussian
   perturbation.

   The identity also explains why the posterior route is close to, but not the
   same as, the smoothed score-max shortcut.  If one could return an additive
   nearest neighbor for \(Z\sim N(q,I_d/t)\), then
   \cref{lem:smoothed-max-score-margin} would already solve the query.  The
   max-tilted law instead asks for the exact-nearest-cell distribution under
   the slightly output-dependent center \(s_\alpha q+\alpha x\), with the
   distance penalty in \((VC2)\).  \Cref{cor:query-centered-cell-reweighting}
   removes even that output-dependent center by size-biasing the exact nearest
   neighbor of a query-centered Gaussian perturbation.  With the
   dimension-aware schedule \(\alpha=\Theta(1/H)\),
   \(H\ge\max\{D,\log m\}\), the size-biasing weight has only
   inverse-polynomial-in-\(\log m\) tail after a polylogarithmic cap.  Hence
   \cref{cor:truncated-nearest-cell-compiler} reduces the posterior sampler to
   repeated exact nearest-cell queries plus a scalar rejection step.  This is a
   more structured sampling problem than arbitrary Gumbel-MIPS over the
   resident points, and a more precise target for code, lattice, trellis, or
   Voronoi-cell quadrature constructions.
\end{remark}

\begin{assumption}[Compact max-tilted leader sampler]
   \label{asm:alpha-leader-sampler}
   Fix \(c>1\) and \(\rho=\rho_c\).  For every bounded-diameter localized node
   with \(m\) resident points there is a retained sampler of space
   \(m^{1+\rho}\log^{O_c(1)}m\) for the max-tilted leader law
   \[
      \Pr[X=x]
      =
      \frac{\widetilde\Lambda_q(x)}
           {\sum_z\widetilde\Lambda_q(z)}
   \]
   from \((AL2)\), either with the logarithmic parameters of
   \cref{lem:scale-amplified-alpha-leader} or with the dimension-aware
   parameters \((QC0)\).  For every promised query \(q\), one sample can be
   produced in
   \(d\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m\) time.
\end{assumption}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional max-tilted leader endpoint]
   \label{prop:alpha-leader-polylog}
   Under \cref{asm:alpha-leader-sampler}, worst-case Euclidean ANN has query
   time
   \[
      Q(n,d)=d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n
   \]
   and space
   \[
      S(n,d)=dn+n^{1+\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n .
   \]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
   Work after bounded-diameter localization in a copy containing a true near
   pair.  Draw one point from the max-tilted leader sampler and verify it in
   the original metric.  By \((AL3)\) and \((AL4)\), or by
   \cref{cor:query-centered-cell-reweighting} for the dimension-aware schedule,
   the normalized sampler returns a valid \(cr\)-neighbor with probability at
   least \(c_c/C_c\).
   Repeating a constant number of times gives constant success probability.
   The local query and space bounds are exactly those assumed, and summing
   over the standard localized copies gives the displayed global bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{assumption}[Compact ideal likelihood leader-law sampler]
   \label{asm:ideal-likelihood-leader-law-sampler}
   Fix \(c>1\) and \(\rho=\rho_c\).  For every bounded-diameter localized node
   with \(m\) resident points there is a retained sampler of space
   \(m^{1+\rho}\log^{O_c(1)}m\) for the ideal likelihood leader laws
   \(\Lambda_{q,\xi}\) of \((LI)\), at dyadic scales
   \[
      \xi\in[1/m,\eta_m],
      \qquad
      \eta_m=m^{-\rho}\log^{A_c}m .
   \]
   For every promised query \(q\) and scale \(\xi\), the query routine can
   compute \(W_{q,\xi}=\sum_x\Lambda_{q,\xi}(x)\) and, when \(W_{q,\xi}>0\),
   sample a point \(X\in S\) from the normalized point law
   \[
      \Pr[X=x]=\Lambda_{q,\xi}(x)/W_{q,\xi}
   \]
   in \(d\log^{O_c(1)}m\) time.
\end{assumption}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional ideal leader-law endpoint]
   \label{prop:ideal-leader-law-polylog}
   Under \cref{asm:ideal-likelihood-leader-law-sampler}, worst-case Euclidean
   ANN has query time
   \[
      Q(n,d)=d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n
   \]
   and space
   \[
      S(n,d)=dn+n^{1+\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n .
   \]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
   Work after bounded-diameter localization.  At a localized node of size
   \(m\), first use a uniform resident guard of size
   \(m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m\).  If the query has at least
   \(\eta_m m/2\) valid resident answers, this guard succeeds with constant
   probability after increasing the logarithmic factor.

   Otherwise, run the ideal leader-law sampler at every dyadic
   \(\xi\le\eta_m\).  At scale \(\xi\), perform
   \[
      T_\xi=\left\lceil C_c\xi^{-\rho}\right\rceil
   \]
   independent Poissonized point-law groups.  A group draws
   \(N\sim\operatorname{Poisson}(W_{q,\xi})\), samples \(N\) points from
   \(\Lambda_{q,\xi}/W_{q,\xi}\), and verifies the distinct sampled points.
   This is \cref{lem:poissonized-normalized-leader-sampler} applied with the
   label family \(S\), leader map \(\ell(x)=x\), and intensities
   \(\Lambda_{q,\xi}(x)\).

   If \(p\) is a true near point in this localized copy, the guard-failure
   alternative in \cref{lem:ideal-likelihood-leader-law} gives
   \((ILL2)\).  Therefore, after the repetitions, the total Poisson intensity
   of valid reported leaders is at least a positive constant, and choosing the
   constant in \(T_\xi\) large enough makes the success probability bounded
   below by a constant.  Verification rejects invalid samples.

   By \((ILL1)\), one group has expected \(O_c(1)\) point samples and
   \(d\log^{O_c(1)}m\) arithmetic work.  Hence the total local expected query
   work is
   \[
      \sum_{\xi\le\eta_m}T_\xi\,d\log^{O_c(1)}m
      \le d\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m .
   \]
   The expected number of verified candidates obeys the same bound.  The
   retained local space is the sampler space from the assumption plus raw
   coordinates; summing over the standard localized copies gives the displayed
   global bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{assumption}[Multiscale normalized likelihood leader sampler]
   \label{asm:normalized-likelihood-leader-sampler}
   Fix \(c>1\) and \(\rho=\rho_c\).  For every bounded-diameter localized node
   with \(m\) resident points there is a retained multiscale leader sampler of
   space \(m^{1+\rho}\log^{O_c(1)}m\).  It uses the dyadic scales
   \[
      \xi\in[1/m,\eta_m],
      \qquad
      \eta_m=m^{-\rho}\log^{A_c}m,
   \]
   and at scale \(\xi\) has an implicit hidden label family
   \(\mathcal A_\xi\).  Each hidden label \(a\in\mathcal A_\xi\) has an
   analysis set \(C_a\subseteq S\), mass
   \(\mu_a=|C_a|/m\in[\xi/2,\xi]\), a leader
   \(\ell(a)\in C_a\), and a query intensity \(\lambda_q(a)\).

   For every promised query \(q\) and scale \(\xi\), the retained query
   routine can compute
   \[
      W_{q,\xi}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal A_\xi}\lambda_q(a)
   \]
   and sample a hidden label from
   \(\lambda_q(a)/W_{q,\xi}\), returning its leader, in
   \(d\log^{O_c(1)}m\) time per sample.  The total one-group intensity is
   bounded by
   \[
      W_{q,\xi}\le\log^{O_c(1)}m .
      \tag{NLS1}
   \]
   Finally, for every near pair \((p,q)\) in the node, either a uniform
   resident guard of size \(m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m\) finds a valid
   \(cr\)-neighbor with constant probability, or the pure multiscale value is
   inverse-polylogarithmic:
   \[
      \sum_{\xi\le\eta_m}
      \sum_{\substack{a\in\mathcal A_\xi\\
                      p\in C_a,\ C_a\subseteq\Ball(q,cr)}}
      \lambda_q(a)\mu_a^{-\rho}
      \ge
      \log^{-O_c(1)}m .
      \tag{NLS2}
   \]
\end{assumption}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional normalized leader-sampler endpoint]
   \label{prop:normalized-leader-sampler-polylog}
   Under \cref{asm:normalized-likelihood-leader-sampler}, worst-case Euclidean
   ANN has query time
   \[
      Q(n,d)=d\,n^{\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n
   \]
   and space
   \[
      S(n,d)=dn+n^{1+\rho_c}\log^{O_c(1)}n .
   \]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
   Work after the projected bounded-diameter localization of
   \cref{lem:localization}.  At a localized node of size \(m\), first try the
   resident guard.  If it does not find a valid point, set
   \(h=\log^{-O_c(1)}m\) to be the lower bound in \((NLS2)\).  For every
   dyadic scale \(\xi\le\eta_m\), perform
   \[
      T_\xi=\left\lceil2^{\rho+1}\xi^{-\rho}h^{-1}\right\rceil
   \]
   independent Poissonized leader-sampler groups.  In one group, use
   \cref{lem:poissonized-normalized-leader-sampler}: draw
   \(N\sim\operatorname{Poisson}(W_{q,\xi})\), draw \(N\) hidden labels from
   the normalized law supplied by the assumption, report their distinct
   leaders, and verify every reported point in the original metric.

   The success proof is the same scale-aware calculation as in
   \cref{lem:dyadic-leader-quotient}.  Let \(V_\xi\) be the contribution of
   scale \(\xi\) to the pure value in \((NLS2)\), and let \(\Lambda_\xi\) be
   the one-group intensity of pure labels at that scale.  Since
   \(\mu_a\ge\xi/2\),
   \[
      \Lambda_\xi\ge2^{-\rho}\xi^\rho V_\xi .
   \]
   Across all repetitions, the total number of activated pure labels is
   Poisson with mean at least
   \[
      \sum_\xi T_\xi\Lambda_\xi
      \ge
      2h^{-1}\sum_\xi V_\xi
      \ge2 .
   \]
   Hence some pure label is activated with probability at least \(1-e^{-2}\).
   Its leader lies in \(C_a\subseteq\Ball(q,cr)\), so verification succeeds.

   For work, \((NLS1)\) and
   \cref{lem:poissonized-normalized-leader-sampler} give expected
   \(d\log^{O_c(1)}m\) cost per group.  Therefore
   \[
      \sum_{\xi\le\eta_m}T_\xi\,d\log^{O_c(1)}m
      \le
      d\,m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m .
   \]
   The expected number of verified candidates satisfies the same bound.  The
   local retained space is the sampler space from the assumption plus the raw
   coordinates, and the standard localization accounting gives the displayed
   global space and query bounds.
\end{proof}

\Cref{asm:ideal-likelihood-leader-law-sampler} is the exact scale-resolved
continuous target: all geometric and scale arithmetic has been discharged in
\cref{lem:ideal-likelihood-leader-law}, and the implementation only has to
sample from the normalized point measures \(\Lambda_{q,\xi}\).  But
\cref{lem:scale-amplified-alpha-leader} shows that even the scale resolution is
not intrinsic after amplification: the dyadic weights collapse to the single
max-tilted leader law \((AL2)\).  Thus the sharpest current posterior target is
\cref{asm:alpha-leader-sampler}.  The query-centered form then weakens the
algorithmic target further: \cref{lem:smoothed-witness-list-compiler} does not
need exact max-tilted sampling or exact Voronoi-cell decoding.  It only needs a
short list that contains the hidden near witness with constant mass under the
tilted Gaussian law \((WL0)\), after which scalar rejection and metric
verification finish the query.  \Cref{lem:poisson-rate-coverage} gives the
corresponding redundancy certificate: multi-assign each point to labels with
budget \(a_x\), probe a query-dependent label set, and pay only the closed-form
tilted miss \(\exp(-a_p\mu_p(B))\) plus the reported incidence load.  The
hidden-label assumption
\cref{asm:normalized-likelihood-leader-sampler} remains a more general
implementation normal form for code, lattice, product-panel, or trellis
samplers that expose a partition function \(W_{q,\xi}\) and sample a tilted
path before quotienting to its leader, exactly as in
\cref{lem:compressed-aggregate-sampler}.

Choose $\eta_m=m^{-\rho}\log^{A_c}m$. A uniform guard of size $m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m$ handles the case where at least $\eta_m m/2$ points are already valid answers. Otherwise \cref{thm:continuous-likelihood-balls} supplies constant pure negative-moment value below mass $\eta_m$, and \cref{lem:ideal-likelihood-scale-profile} shows that the same ideal continuum already has the scale-normalized query and storage moments needed by \cref{lem:dyadic-structured-amplification}. \Cref{lem:ideal-likelihood-leader-law} packages these facts in the quotient language: each scale has only constant total leader intensity, and the scale-weighted valid-leader intensity is constant under guard failure. The value need not sit at the top scale $\eta_m$; it may be concentrated near singleton children. This is why the amplification lemma runs all dyadic scales down to $1/m$. The geometric cost of doing so is still only $m^\rho\log^{O_c(1)}m$.

Thus the remaining posterior problem is no longer geometric. By \cref{lem:likelihood-rank-prefix}, it is also not inherently a finite-channel quantization problem: after the Gaussian score \(Y\) is fixed, the threshold continuum collapses to score-prefix rank gaps. By \cref{cor:likelihood-prefix-leader}, even those prefixes do not need separate materialized representatives for ANN; a pure prefix certifies that the score leader for the same \(Y\) is already valid. \Cref{lem:dyadic-leader-quotient} gives the corresponding compiler, and \cref{cor:likelihood-leader-intensity} identifies the exact quotient law: sample or report data points from the aggregate score-leader intensities \((LI)\), where the rank-prefix gaps appear only as weights. By \cref{lem:poissonized-normalized-leader-sampler}, an implementation does not have to list active hidden labels either; it is enough to compute the scale partition function and sample from the normalized rank-gap law. After applying the scale repetitions analytically, \cref{lem:scale-amplified-alpha-leader} collapses these normalized laws to one max-tilted leader sampler. Uniformly choosing a representative from the whole node would lose a factor equal to the prefix mass, but the rank-prefix sensitivity is now encoded in the max tilt \(M_Y^\alpha\), not in stored posterior buckets.

There is an even more collapsed non-channel target. Taking the likelihood exponent to the $\alpha=0$ endpoint turns the posterior child family into one smoothed score order. Then it is enough to draw a point from the high-score part of that order; no threshold continuum, dyadic scale, or stored representative is needed.

\begin{lemma}[Smoothed maximum-score margin]\label{lem:smoothed-max-score-margin}
   Let $S\subset\R^d$, $|S|=m$, and suppose $\|p-q\|\le r$. For
   \[
      A_Y(x)=\ip{Y}{x}-\frac t2\|x\|^2,
      \qquad
      Y\sim N(tq,tI_d),
   \]
   choose any
   \[
      t\ge C_c\frac{\log m}{r^2}
   \]
   with $C_c$ sufficiently large. Then, with probability at least
   $1-m^{-2}$ over $Y$, every invalid point $x\in S$ with
   $\|x-q\|>cr$ satisfies
   \[
      A_Y(x)\le A_Y(p)-\delta_c t r^2
   \]
   for a constant $\delta_c>0$ depending only on $c$. Consequently, on this
